{"parentUuid":"d42b27d6-ed8e-4998-9449-a9f741062fec","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a35f1a5a89ceb1af1","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- artifact-planner: Produce a self-contained, navigable HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPLIT STORYBOARD: each scene pairs a rich UI/mockup (left) with a before→after Mermaid diagram of the structural change (right), plus a \"what this lets you do\" line and an \"under the hood\" strip. Embeds a machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, walk through, or visualize any multi-step flow: onboarding, checkout, auth, OAuth integrations, wizards, subscription cancellation, push notifications, support ticket lifecycles, AI chat execution, CI/CD pipelines, refund flows, admin features, migrations, webhook lifecycles, or anything else with sequenced stages and decision points. Trigger on \"create an artifact for…\", \"plan this flow\", \"make a flow plan\", \"visualize this flow\", \"show me the stages of…\", \"mock up the flow for…\", \"I want to plan X visually\", \"build a walkthrough of…\", or any time the user has just described a feature/flow and would benefit from a single-page visual + structured spec they can revisit, share, or drop back into a new chat. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- artifact-planner-canvas: Produce a self-contained HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPATIAL CANVAS: the WHOLE plan on one pannable/zoomable Figma-style board — frontend mockups in a top band, each stage's before→after Mermaid diagram directly beneath (dashed spine connectors), branches as tinted lanes with fork/rejoin curves, decisions and open questions as sticky notes placed next to the stages they affect, and double-click-anywhere comment stickies. This is the CANVAS variant of `artifact-planner` (which pages one scene at a time as a split storyboard): prefer the canvas when the user wants to see everything at once, compare stages spatially, or leave free-floating comments; prefer the paged storyboard for linear walkthroughs and presentations. Embeds the same machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, or visualize any multi-step flow (onboarding, checkout, auth, wizards, CI/CD pipelines, migrations, webhook lifecycles…) AND signals a canvas: \"canvas artifact\", \"spatial plan\", \"put the plan on a canvas/board\", \"Figma-style board\", \"show me the whole flow at once\", \"map it out spatially\" — or when they ask for the canvas version of an existing artifact-plan. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- auto-rough-cut: Turn a folder of raw class-video recordings — separately recorded pristine voice tracks (Audacity .wav) plus their screen/camera videos (.mp4) — into a draggable Adobe Premiere Pro FCP7 XML that is silence-cut to Ray's Recut preferences, has the pristine audio synced and linked to the video, transcribes every kept chunk, and flags repeated takes / filler / breaths in PURPLE for a fast review pass. Use this skill whenever Ray points at a folder of recording pairs and wants a rough cut, first assembly, or \"silence cut\", or says things like \"cut the silences\", \"sync my audio and video and remove the dead air\", \"make a Premiere XML from these recordings\", \"apply my Recut process / record preferences\", \"flag the retakes\", \"auto rough-cut this video\", \"turn my Audacity audio + screen recording into a timeline\", or stages new recordings under a \"[Class Videos]\" / to-film folder. Trigger it even if Recut, transcription, or retakes aren't all named — any \"remove the silence and give me a Premiere XML I can still drag around\" request for paired audio+video is this skill. Distinct from recut-xml-fixer (which only repairs an XML Recut already exported); this skill does the whole cut from raw media.\n- auto-spec: Turn a feature brief (inline text or a stub plan file) into an implementation-ready plan via an autonomous two-agent interview, an external-API research verification pass, and a two-agent codebase-grounded planning pass — and, in its bootstrap mode, build/refresh the personalized digital twin that powers that interview by mining your Claude Code conversation history. Use when the user says \"auto spec\", \"spec autopilot\", \"auto interview\", \"flesh out this plan\", \"/auto-spec <file or brief>\", or otherwise wants a plan written without manually answering questions; ALSO use for \"create my auto-spec\", \"clone my brain\", \"make a spec twin\", \"personalize auto-spec\", \"build my decision profile\", or any request to analyze conversation history for decision patterns. Produces a single plan file with Feature Brief, Spec Decisions, External Research, Codebase Map, and phased Implementation Plan — ready to hand to `/implement-task` — or, in bootstrap mode, a refreshed `decision_profile.md` + `qa_examples.json` digital twin.\n- chronicle\n- codex-consult: OpenAI Codex CLI wrapper — three modes. Code review: independent diff review via\ncodex review with pass/fail gate. Challenge: adversarial mode that tries to break\nyour code. Consult: ask codex anything with session continuity for follow-ups.\nThe \"200 IQ autistic developer\" second opinion. Use when asked to \"codex review\",\n\"codex challenge\", \"ask codex\", \"second opinion\", or \"consult codex\".\n- improve-claude-md: improve a CLAUDE.md file using <important if> blocks to improve instruction adherence\n- interview: Relentlessly interrogate a plan, decision, or idea one question at a time until the user and Claude reach a genuinely shared understanding. Use this whenever the user wants to stress-test their thinking, pressure-test a plan, be challenged on an idea, or says any of \"interview me\", \"grill me\", \"grill this\", \"poke holes in this\", \"stress-test this\", \"ask me everything\", \"interrogate me\", \"what am I missing\", or \"/interview\". Also reach for it when the user drops a half-formed plan and asks for questions before you build anything, or when a request is underspecified enough that guessing would waste real work. Do not write code, files, or plans while this skill is active until the user says the picture is complete.\n- slackbot-message: Send Slack messages to a named channel via a bot user. Use this skill whenever you need to notify Ray on Slack, post a status update, alert a channel, deliver a short summary, or when he asks you to \"post to slack\", \"message #channel\", \"send a slack message\", \"ping slack\", \"slack me\", \"let me know on slack\", or names a specific Slack channel by name. Also use it after long-running tasks finish if Ray asked for Slack notifications. The bot is invited to the relevant channels, so the script can post to any channel by its human-readable name (no need to know channel IDs).\n- spec-developer-v2: Interview the user about a rough idea or brief with sharp, non-obvious product and technical questions, then write a concise implementation-ready spec. Use when the user wants to spec something out, develop a vague idea into requirements, or turn a product concept into a detailed plan.\n- sync-skills: Commit and push GLOBAL skill changes to the personal skills git repo\n(github.com/ray-amjad/private-skills). Auto-detects what changed in\n~/.claude/skills/, stages the changes, generates a meaningful commit message,\nand pushes to origin main. Use this skill whenever you finish creating or\nediting a global skill, or when the user says \"sync skills\", \"push skills\",\n\"save skills\", \"/sync-skills\", or asks to commit skill changes to git.\nIMPORTANT: Only trigger this skill when working at the project level (i.e., when\nthe working directory is a project repo, not from within ~/.claude/skills/ itself).\nDo NOT use this for project-scoped skills (e.g. skills under a project's own\n.claude/skills/, like ray-os's sentence-mining) — those are committed to that\nproject's repo, not here.\n- ultragoal\n- vercel-logs: Check Vercel production logs, errors, and build output. Use this skill whenever you need to\ndiagnose a deployment error, check for runtime errors, investigate 500s, or the user asks\n\"check the logs\", \"what's the error\", \"why is it broken\", \"check vercel logs\", or any\nrequest to look at Vercel deployment/runtime/build logs. Also trigger on `/vercel-logs`.\n\nCRITICAL: Also invoke this skill BEFORE running any `vercel logs` command yourself —\neven when no one asked you to. The CLI has non-obvious flag interactions (implicit\nfollow mode, stderr-only output, --since rejection) that cause hangs or silent failures\nif you guess at flags. If you're about to type `vercel logs` in a Bash call, load this\nskill first.\n- vercel-monitor: Watch a Vercel deployment in the background and notify when it reaches Ready/Error/Canceled.\nUse this skill whenever the user pushes to main, runs `git push`, deploys to Vercel, or asks\nto \"monitor the deploy\", \"watch the build\", \"check if it deployed\", or anything similar —\neven if they don't explicitly say \"Vercel\". Also trigger on `/vercel-monitor`. This replaces\nolder patterns of spawning a haiku subagent or polling via the Vercel MCP, both of which\nwaste tokens and context on large projects.\n- anki-cleanup\n- binary-explorer: Reverse-engineer any desktop application OR Claude Code's own binary to understand how it works behind the scenes — the APIs it calls, the internal flow, the models/providers it uses, its feature flags, config constants, embedded prompts, IPC protocols. For general apps it works on Electron apps (Wispr Flow, Slack, Discord, VS Code, Cursor, Notion, Linear…), native macOS .app bundles, single-executable bundles (Bun/Node/Deno/Go SEA), and raw Mach-O / ELF / PE binaries. It ALSO reverse-engineers Claude Code's own binary — it knows Claude Code's mangled variable conventions, feature-flag patterns, and embedded-prompt layout. Use this skill whenever the user points at a desktop app and wants to know \"how does X work under the hood\", \"what API does it call\", \"how does it do Y\", \"what's the flow for Z\", \"reverse engineer this app\", \"figure out how this feature works\", \"what endpoints does it hit\", \"does it run locally or in the cloud\", \"what model does it use\", or names a specific app path (.app, .exe, a binary). ALSO use it whenever the user asks about a Claude Code feature, feature flag, hidden toggle, internal prompt, or wants to understand how something in Claude Code works under the hood — especially unreleased, gated, or undocumented functionality — or says \"how does X work in the binary\", \"what does this feature flag do\", \"find the prompt for X\", \"reverse engineer X\", or mentions a specific codename/flag. Also trigger when the user wants to audit an app's network behavior, understand its architecture, or ext…\n- class-scriptwriter: Turn a raw idea, source tweet, video transcript, feature announcement, or rough outline into a polished video script for Ray's Agentic Coding School classes. Use this skill whenever the user says \"write a class script\", \"turn this into a class video\", \"script this for the [business/claude-code/prompt-engineering/skills/context-engineering/techniques/claude-chat/claude-cowork/correction] class\", \"draft an agentic coding school video\", or whenever the user is staging content in `/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os/projects/agentic-coding-school/to-film/` and wants it written up. Default to this skill for any new file destined for the `to-film/` tree, even if the user does not name it explicitly. Produces an essay-style markdown script with the correct frontmatter, horizontal rules between sections, inline image embed placeholders, and a Demo section, written in Ray's direct second-person voice.\n- content-cascade\n- discord-archive\n- excalidraw-bundle: Bundle a set of section images into a single .excalidraw file laid out as a grid — one column per section in document order, the section's variations stacked vertically — and save it to ~/Downloads, ready to drag into excalidraw.com or the Obsidian Excalidraw plugin.\n- excalidraw-codex: Generate excalidraw-style explanation images using the Codex CLI (codex exec --json) instead of a paid API. Use when the user wants visual explanations, diagrams, or illustrations in the hand-drawn excalidraw aesthetic and asks for the codex path. Codex's bundled image_gen tool runs through the logged-in account, so no GEMINI_API_KEY is required and it's often faster than the Gemini route. Triggers on \"generate an excalidraw image\", \"excalidraw-codex\", \"use codex for excalidraw\", or any request for excalidraw-style diagrams where no engine is named. This is the RECOMMENDED DEFAULT path for excalidraw image generation: it needs no API key and runs through the logged-in Codex account. Sibling skill [[excalidraw-gemini]] does the same job through the paid Gemini API. Default to codex whenever the user does not explicitly ask for the Gemini route.\n- excalidraw-codex-simple\n- excalidraw-deck: Build hand-drawn, Excalidraw-style HTML slide decks that explain a concept through sketch-aesthetic vector diagrams — rough.js strokes on a dark canvas, full-bleed, one diagram per slide with a caption underneath, arrow-key and tap navigation. Use this skill whenever the user wants explainer slides, a concept deck, diagram slides, or a talk/video presentation in the hand-drawn sketch look, or says things like \"make slides for X\", \"build a deck for X\", \"add a slide in this style\", \"turn this into a sketch/excalidraw-style deck\", \"another slide like the workflow deck\", or points at an existing rough.js deck and wants more in the same style. This is for self-contained vector-diagram decks — NOT the Claude Code terminal-aesthetic slides (use cc-terminal-slides) and NOT raster image generation (use excalidraw-gen, which calls an image model). Reach for this skill proactively for any \"explain this concept on slides\" request, even when the user does not say the word \"excalidraw\".\n- extract-wisdom: Extract structured wisdom, insights, and actionable takeaways from any content — podcasts, articles, videos, transcripts, books, or conversations. Use this skill whenever the user asks to \"extract wisdom\", \"summarize insights\", \"pull out key ideas\", \"what are the takeaways\", \"extract the best parts\", \"distill this content\", \"what did I learn from this\", or provides a transcript/article and asks for a structured breakdown. Also trigger when the user pastes or references long-form content and wants the signal extracted from the noise — even if they don't use the word \"wisdom\" specifically. Inspired by Daniel Miessler's Fabric pattern. Has a BATCH MODE for running this extraction across a whole YouTube channel in bulk: lists videos with yt-dlp, filters by duration, downloads transcripts, then fans out one extract-wisdom subagent per video in batches of 5, writing one wisdom file per video to disk. Trigger batch mode whenever the user wants wisdom/insights/takeaways from a YouTube channel in bulk: \"extract wisdom from this channel\", \"go through @<handle>'s long videos\", \"summarise everything on <channel>\", \"give me the best ideas from <creator>'s podcast\", \"process a whole channel into wisdom notes\", or \"I want to read the highlights from every video on @<handle> longer than 20 minutes\".\n- humanizer\n- idea-foundry\n- image-cleanup: Clean up unreferenced images in Obsidian vault folders. Deletes unused images, renames excalidraw files with descriptive names based on image content (using vision), and moves everything to vault root images/ folder. Use after picking favorites from excalidraw-gen batches. Triggers on \"clean up images\", \"delete unused images\", \"tidy up images\", \"image cleanup\", or \"/image-cleanup\".\n- linkedin: All LinkedIn tasks — writing posts (text or carousel), scouting sources to post about, checking post performance, browsing the feed, or analyzing competitors. Triggers on any mention of LinkedIn, including \"write a LinkedIn post\", \"make a LinkedIn carousel\", \"swipeable LinkedIn post\", \"LinkedIn slides\", \"check my LinkedIn\", \"how did my posts do\", \"LinkedIn competitors\", \"post on LinkedIn\", \"browse LinkedIn feed\". Also triggers on \"check post engagement\", \"update post metrics\", or reviewing social media performance. Also use it for finding something to post about — \"find me a LinkedIn topic\", \"run a source sweep\", \"scout me something on MCP\", \"what should I post today\", or reviewing the drafts the daily scout left in Slack\n- newsletter-writer: Write personal newsletters in a conversational, essay-style format inspired by Ali Abdaal's style. Use when the user wants to write a newsletter issue, draft an email to their list, write a LifeNotes-style essay, or asks for help with newsletter writing. Triggers on requests like \"write a newsletter\", \"draft a newsletter about...\", \"help me write my weekly email\", \"newsletter style\", or when the user provides a topic and wants it turned into a newsletter format.\n- rakuten-hotels\n- recut-xml-fixer: Reshape an FCP7 XML exported from a silence-cutting app (Recut, AutoEdit, Vika, etc.) so Adobe Premiere Pro imports it cleanly with the user's pristine external audio (e.g. Audio.wav from Audacity) linked to the video instead of the camera/screen-recording's own audio. Use this skill whenever the user points at an .xml or sequence file from Recut, AutoEdit, or any auto-cutter and wants to swap in their own audio track, drop the redundant on-camera audio, fix vertical alignment between video and audio cuts, or fix a Premiere \"import failed\" error on a Recut-style XML. Triggers on phrases like \"fix this Recut XML\", \"swap the audio in this premiere XML\", \"align the cuts to my Audio.wav\", \"remove the loom audio track\", \"merge V1 and A1 in this XML so they're linked\", or any reference to a workflow combining a silence-cutter app's XML with a separately recorded pristine audio file.\n- sentence-mining: Build and maintain Japanese sentence-mining cards for Anki, fully self-contained (no AnkiMorphs install required) and configurable per user via a one-time `/sentence-mining setup`. Six modes, and the skill ASKS which one you want on every launch. (1) Video mode — paste any Instagram reel, YouTube video/Short, TikTok, Twitter video, or local file → yt-dlp + AssemblyAI + SudachiPy + a built-in i+1 known-word diff produces draft cards. (2) Bank mode — give a list of target words → find the best example sentence for each through one canonical cascade (Immersion Kit → Nadeshiko → sentencesearch → kotu → your locally-indexed subs2srs .apkg banks), re-ranked by your own i+1, with native audio and a screenshot where the source ships one. (3) Replace mode — fix existing cards whose example sentence is bad (too short, a fragment, incomprehensible): pull a better sentence through the same cascade, edit the card in place (archiving the old sentence to a previous_versions field), and rehabilitate the card so you re-learn it fresh. (4) Leech mode — work the cards Anki flagged as leeches, but filtered by whether they are STILL failing: `tag:leech` is a scar Anki never removes, so most of a leech list is usually cards that recovered months ago (43 of Ray's 78 sat at 21-338 day intervals). Cards below the interval threshold get a fresh sentence through the cascade plus full rehabilitation (reps/lapses zeroed, due today); recovered cards just have the tag stripped with their scheduling untouched; words rarer than a JPDB frequen…\n- transcribe-youtube-video: Transcribe audio or video into a clean .txt + timestamped .srt + YouTube chapter timestamps. Re-encodes any WAV/MP4 to a small MP3 before upload, mines on-screen text with OCR to seed AssemblyAI key terms, runs AssemblyAI Universal-3 Pro speech-to-text, then generates punchy chapter markers from the SRT. Use whenever the user wants to transcribe a video or audio file, generate subtitles/captions or an SRT, create a YouTube transcript or chapters, or convert a recording to text. Also trigger when the user has a WAV/MP4/MOV and wants text out of it, even without saying 'transcribe'.\n- ungate-claude-code-feature: Enable a gated, experimental, or staged-rollout Claude Code feature on your own machine by finding its GrowthBook feature flag in the binary and force-enabling it through a Proxyman interception rule (with an optional disk-cache fast-path). Use this skill whenever the user wants to ungate, unlock, force-enable, or \"turn on\" a Claude Code feature hidden behind a flag — phrasings like \"ungate the X feature\", \"enable ultrareview\", \"unlock [feature name]\", \"turn on [feature]\", \"enable the workflow/agent tool\", \"this feature won't show up even with the env var\", \"force-enable a tengu_ flag\", \"flip the flag for X\", \"intercept the feature flag\", \"unlock a beta Claude Code feature\", or any case where a Claude Code feature is gated and the user wants it on. Also trigger when the user references a specific tengu_ flag name, or the existing \"GrowthBook Flag Enabler\" Proxyman rule, or wants to add another flag to it. For the user's own Claude Code install and account.\n- watch-next-jp\n- wisdom-to-acs-gap: Turn ONE video (YouTube URL/id or a pasted transcript) into an Agentic Coding School (ACS) content-gap report: extract its full wisdom, promote the 1-3 load-bearing \"spine\" ideas, deep-dive each in prose, gap-check each against the live ACS catalog, and pitch the buildable new ACS videos. The analysis engine behind the acs/gap-scout routine, but also runs standalone. Use this skill WHENEVER the user pastes or links a coding/AI video (podcast, talk, tutorial, feature walkthrough) and wants the ACS angle: \"what ACS video could come from this\", \"run the gap scout on this\", \"gap-check this against the catalog\", \"find the spine idea\", \"is this already covered in the school\", \"what should I film from this\", \"mine this video for ACS\", or just drops a transcript/link and asks what's video-worthy. Also the per-video worker the gap-scout routine calls for each swept video. Do NOT use for a bulk channel sweep (that is the gap-scout routine / youtube-outlier-research routine) or for plain wisdom extraction with no ACS gap-check (that is extract-wisdom).\n- x-article\n- x-thread-miner\n- x-video-promo\n- youtube-ab-tester: End-to-end YouTube A/B testing for Ray's channel (@RAmjad) — title generation via subagent, thumbnail image generation via `gemini-3-pro-image-preview` at 1K, results recording, and the underlying scripts/golden references all live here. Use whenever the user wants to brainstorm titles, generate thumbnails (text or images), record A/B test results, analyze title/thumbnail performance, or asks things like \"suggest titles\", \"what titles should I test\", \"make a thumbnail\", \"thumbnail variations\", \"5 thumbnails for this video\", \"Matt style\", \"Nate style\", \"record these results\", \"what's working\", or \"update the A/B test results\". Also trigger when the user shares a screenshot of YouTube A/B test results and wants analysis or recording. Do NOT use for competitor title research (the youtube-outlier-research routine covers niche/competitor research) or video scripting (use youtube-scriptwriter instead).\n- youtube-miner\n- youtube-writer\n- review-plan\n- find-prompt-injections\n- loop-opportunities\n- loop-opportunities-personas\n- loop-opportunities-topdown\n- loopy-ai-class-review\n- loopy-ai-source-enrich\n- loopy-ai-stub-walk\n- mine-video-batch\n- claude-md-management:revise-claude-md\n- skill-creator:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- mermaid-diagram:mermaid-diagram-generator: Generate interactive HTML diagrams with Mermaid.js for visualizing flows, sequences, architectures, and processes. Use when the user asks to \"create a diagram\", \"visualize the flow\", \"draw a sequence diagram\", \"show me how X works\", or needs visual documentation of system interactions, workflows, database schemas, or technical processes.\n- frontend-design:frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- claude-md-management:claude-md-improver\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- claude-in-chrome: Automates your Chrome browser to interact with web pages - clicking elements, filling forms, capturing screenshots, reading console logs, and navigating sites. Opens pages in new tabs within your existing Chrome session. Requires site-level permissions before executing (configured in the extension). - When the user wants to interact with web pages, automate browser tasks, capture screenshots, read console logs, or perform any browser-based actions. Always invoke BEFORE attempting to use any mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools.\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init\n- review: Review a GitHub pull request; Without these in allowHeaders the preflight blocks the\n19\t// POST before it reaches the route (X-STT-* are non-simple request headers).\n20\tapp.use('*', cors({\n21\t  origin: '*',\n22\t  allowHeaders: ['Content-Type', 'X-STT-Provider', 'X-STT-Model', 'X-STT-Domain'],\n23\t  allowMethods: ['GET', 'POST', 'OPTIONS'],\n24\t}));\n25\t\n26\tapp.options('*', (c) => c.body(null, 204));\n27\t\n28\t// Health check endpoint - Fly.io uses this for health monitoring\n29\tapp.get('/health', (c) => {\n30\t  return c.json({\n31\t    status: 'ok',\n32\t    region: process.env.FLY_REGION || 'local',\n33\t    timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),\n34\t  });\n35\t});\n36\t\n37\t// Warmup endpoint - clients hit this on hotkey-down to pre-establish\n38\t// the TLS/HTTP2 connection before the /transcribe POST.\n39\tapp.get('/warmup', (c) => {\n40\t  c.header('Cache-Control', 'no-store');\n41\t  return c.body(null, 204);\n42\t});\n43\t\n44\t// Main transcription endpoint\n45\tapp.post('/transcribe', transcribeRoute);\n46\t\n47\t// Standalone post-processing endpoint\n48\tapp.post('/post-process', postProcessRoute);\n49\t\n50\t// Assistant mode endpoint (vision LLM)\n51\tapp.post('/assistant', assistantRoute);\n52\t\n53\t// Usage endpoint\n54\tapp.get('/usage', usageRoute);\n55\t\n56\t// WebSocket streaming endpoint\n57\tapp.get('/ws/streaming-deepgram', wsStreamingPreflight, wsStreamingRoute);\n58\t\n59\t// Fallback - match Cloudflare (405, plain text)\n60\tapp.notFound((c) => {\n61\t  return c.text('Method not allowed', 405);\n62\t});\n63\t\n64\t// Error handler — never echo raw err.message to the client (it can contain\n65\t// env-var names, upstream provider bodies, request IDs). Log the full error\n66\t// server-side with an error_id the client can quote to support.\n67\tapp.onError((err, c) => {\n68\t  const errorId = crypto.randomUUID();\n69\t  console.error(`Unhandled error [${errorId}]:`, err);\n70\t  return c.json({\n71\t    error: 'Internal server error',\n72\t    message: 'An unexpected error occurred. Without these in allowHeaders the preflight blocks the\n// POST before it reaches the route (X-STT-* are non-simple request headers).\napp.use('*', cors({\n  origin: '*',\n  allowHeaders: ['Content-Type', 'X-STT-Provider', 'X-STT-Model', 'X-STT-Domain'],\n  allowMethods: ['GET', 'POST', 'OPTIONS'],\n}));\n\napp.options('*', (c) => c.body(null, 204));\n\n// Health check endpoint - Fly.io uses this for health monitoring\napp.get('/health', (c) => {\n  return c.json({\n    status: 'ok',\n    region: process.env.FLY_REGION || 'local',\n    timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),\n  });\n});\n\n// Warmup endpoint - clients hit this on hotkey-down to pre-establish\n// the TLS/HTTP2 connection before the /transcribe POST.\napp.get('/warmup', (c) => {\n  c.header('Cache-Control', 'no-store');\n  return c.body(null, 204);\n});\n\n// Main transcription endpoint\napp.post('/transcribe', transcribeRoute);\n\n// Standalone post-processing endpoint\napp.post('/post-process', postProcessRoute);\n\n// Assistant mode endpoint (vision LLM)\napp.post('/assistant', assistantRoute);\n\n// Usage endpoint\napp.get('/usage', usageRoute);\n\n// WebSocket streaming endpoint\napp.get('/ws/streaming-deepgram', wsStreamingPreflight, wsStreamingRoute);\n\n// Fallback - match Cloudflare (405, plain text)\napp.notFound((c) => {\n  return c.text('Method not allowed', 405);\n});\n\n// Error handler — never echo raw err.message to the client (it can contain\n// env-var names, upstream provider bodies, request IDs). Log the full error\n// server-side with an error_id the client can quote to support.\napp.onError((err, c) => {\n  const errorId = crypto.randomUUID();\n  console.error(`Unhandled error [${errorId}]:`, err);\n  return c.json({\n    error: 'Internal server error',\n    message: 'An unexpected error occurred. Please try again.',\n    error_id: errorId,\n  }, 500);\n});\n\n// Graceful shutdown: Fly sends SIGTERM on deploy/scale-down with a grace\n// period before SIGKILL (kill_timeout, default 5s). Credit deductions are\n// fired without awaiting after the response is flushed, so drain any that are\n// still in flight before exiting — otherwise the user keeps the transcript\n// but the charge is silently dropped.\nconst SHUTDOWN_DRAIN_MS = 4_000;\nlet shuttingDown = false;\n\nasync function gracefulShutdown(signal: string): Promise<void> {\n  if (shuttingDown) return;\n  shuttingDown = true;\n\n  console.log('machine.shutdown', {\n    signal,\n    machineId: process.env.FLY_MACHINE_ID || 'local',\n    shutdownAt: new Date().toISOString(),\n  });\n\n  const drained = await drainPendingDeductions(SHUTDOWN_DRAIN_MS);\n  if (drained > 0) {\n    console.log('machine.shutdown_drained_deductions', { count: drained });\n  }\n  process.exit(0);\n}\n\nprocess.on('SIGTERM', () => { void gracefulShutdown('SIGTERM'); });\nprocess.on('SIGINT', () => { void gracefulShutdown('SIGINT'); });\n\n// Export for Bun\nexport default {\n  port: Number(process.env.PORT) || 8080,\n  fetch: app.fetch,\n  websocket,\n};\n\nconsole.log('machine.boot', {\n  region: process.env.FLY_REGION || 'local',\n  machineId: process.env.FLY_MACHINE_ID || 'local',\n  allocId: process.env.FLY_ALLOC_ID || undefined,\n  imageRef: process.env.FLY_IMAGE_REF || undefined,\n  port: Number(process.env.PORT) || 8080,\n  bootAt: new Date().toISOString(),\n});\nconsole.log(`HyperWhisper Fly.io service starting on port ${process.env.PORT || 8080}`);\nconsole.log(`Region: ${process.env.FLY_REGION || 'local'}`);\nconsole.log(`License API: ${process.env.NEXTJS_LICENSE_API_URL || 'https://www.hyperwhisper.com (default)'}`);\nconsole.log('Started server: http://localhost:' + (process.env.PORT || 8080));\n","numLines":124,"startLine":1,"totalLines":124}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"37350002-2816-4d9a-8b0b-25295d73111e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

USAGE ROUTE\n2\t// GET /usage - Query credit balance and rate limits\n3\t\n4\timport type { Context } from 'hono';\n5\timport { CREDITS_PER_MINUTE, DEFAULT_API_BASE_URL, LICENSE_API_TIMEOUT_MS } from '../lib/constants';\n6\timport { getClientIP } from '../lib/request-id';\n7\timport { getCachedLicense, cacheLicense } from '../lib/redis';\n8\timport { errorResponse, jsonResponse } from '../lib/responses';\n9\timport { isIPBlocked } from '../lib/redis';\n10\timport { roundToTenth } from '../lib/utils';\n11\t\n12\texport function readFiniteCredits(data: unknown): number | null {\n13\t  if (\n14\t    typeof data === 'object'\n15\t    && data !== null\n16\t    && 'credits' in data\n17\t    && typeof data.credits === 'number'\n18\t    && Number.isFinite(data.credits)\n19\t  ) {\n20\t    return data.credits;\n21\t  }\n22\t\n23\t  return null;\n24\t}\n25\t\n26\tasync function validateLicenseAndGetCredits(licenseKey: string, forceRefresh: boolean): Promise<{ isValid: boolean; credits: number }> {\n27\t  if (!forceRefresh) {\n28\t    const cached = await getCachedLicense(licenseKey);\n29\t    if (cached) {\n30\t      return { isValid: cached.isValid, credits: cached.credits };\n31\t    }\n32\t  }\n33\t\n34\t  const apiBase = (process.env.NEXTJS_LICENSE_API_URL || DEFAULT_API_BASE_URL).replace(/\\/+$/, '');\n35\t\n36\t  try {\n37\t    const response = await fetch(`${apiBase}/api/license/validate`, {\n38\t      method: 'POST',\n39\t      headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },\n40\t      body: JSON.stringify({ license_key: licenseKey, include_credits: true }),\n41\t      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(LICENSE_API_TIMEOUT_MS),\n42\t    });\n43\t\n44\t    const data = await response.json().catch(() => ({})) as { valid?: boolean; credits?: number };\n45\t    const isValid = data.valid === true;\n46\t    const credits = readFiniteCredits(data) ?? 0;\n47\t\n48\t    await cacheLicense(licenseKey, {\n49\t      isValid,\n50\t      credits,\n51\t      cachedAt: new Date().toISOString(),\n52\t    });\n53\t\n54\t    return { isValid, credits };\n55\t  } catch {\n56\t    return { isValid: false, credits: 0 };\n57\t  }\n58\t}\n59\t\n60\tasync function getCreditsBalance(licenseKey: string): Promise<{ credits: number; error?: string }> {\n61\t  const apiBase = (process.env.NEXTJS_LICENSE_API_URL || DEFAULT_API_BASE_URL).replace(/\\/+$/, '');\n62\t\n63\t  try {\n64\t    const response = await fetch(`${apiBase}/api/license/credits?license_key=${encodeURIComponent(licenseKey)}`, {\n65\t      method: 'GET',\n66\t      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(LICENSE_API_TIMEOUT_MS),\n67\t    });\n68\t\n69\t    if (!response.ok) {\n70\t      const errorData = await response.json().catch(() => ({})) as { error?: string };\n71\t      return { credits: 0, error: errorData.error || `HTTP ${response.status}` };\n72\t    }\n73\t\n74\t    const data = await response.json().catch(() => ({}));\n75\t    const credits = readFiniteCredits(data);\n76\t\n77\t    if (credits === null) {\n78\t      return { credits: 0, error: 'Invalid credits response' };\n79\t    }\n80\t\n81\t    await cacheLicense(licenseKey, {\n82\t      isValid: true,\n83\t      credits,\n84\t      cachedAt: new Date().toISOString(),\n85\t    });\n86\t\n87\t    return { credits };\n88\t  } catch (error) {\n89\t    return { credits: 0, error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error) };\n90\t  }\n91\t}\n92\t\n93\texport async function usageRoute(c: Context) {\n94\t  const clientIP = getClientIP(c);\n95\t\n96\t  if (await isIPBlocked(clientIP)) {\n97\t    return errorResponse(403, 'Access denied', 'Your IP has been temporarily blocked due to abuse');\n98\t  }\n99\t\n100\t  // `account_key` is the canonical param; `license_key` is the legacy alias that\n101\t  // installed native apps still send, so we accept either.\n102\t  const licenseKey =\n103\t    c.req.query('account_key') || c.req.query('license_key') || c.req.query('identifier')?.trim() || null;\n104\t  const forceRefresh = c.req.query('force_refresh') === 'true';\n105\t\n106\t  if (licenseKey) {\n107\t    let isValid = false;\n108\t    let credits = 0;\n109\t\n110\t    if (forceRefresh) {\n111\t      const cached = await getCachedLicense(licenseKey);\n112\t      if (cached?.isValid) {\n113\t        const balanceResult = await getCreditsBalance(licenseKey);\n114\t        if (balanceResult.error) {\n115\t          const validation = await validateLicenseAndGetCredits(licenseKey, true);\n116\t          isValid = validation.isValid;\n117\t          credits = validation.credits;\n118\t        } else {\n119\t          isValid = true;\n120\t          credits = balanceResult.credits;\n121\t        }\n122\t      } else {\n123\t        const validation = await validateLicenseAndGetCredits(licenseKey, true);\n124\t        isValid = validation.isValid;\n125\t        credits = validation.credits;\n126\t      }\n127\t    } else {\n128\t      const validation = await validateLicenseAndGetCredits(licenseKey, false);\n129\t      isValid = validation.isValid;\n130\t      credits = validation.credits;\n131\t    }\n132\t\n133\t    if (!isValid) {\n134\t      return errorResponse(401, 'Invalid license key', 'The provided license key is invalid or expired');\n135\t    }\n136\t\n137\t    const normalizedCredits = roundToTenth(credits);\n138\t    const minutesRemaining = Math.floor(normalizedCredits / CREDITS_PER_MINUTE);\n139\t\n140\t    const response = {\n141\t      credits_remaining: normalizedCredits,\n142\t      minutes_remaining: minutesRemaining,\n143\t      credits_per_minute: CREDITS_PER_MINUTE,\n144\t      is_licensed: true,\n145\t      is_trial: credits: number }> {\n  if (!forceRefresh) {\n    const cached = await getCachedLicense(licenseKey);\n    if (cached) {\n      return { isValid: cached.isValid, credits: cached.credits };\n    }\n  }\n\n  const apiBase = (process.env.NEXTJS_LICENSE_API_URL || DEFAULT_API_BASE_URL).replace(/\\/+$/, '');\n\n  try {\n    const response = await fetch(`${apiBase}/api/license/validate`, {\n      method: 'POST',\n      headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },\n      body: JSON.stringify({ license_key: licenseKey, include_credits: true }),\n      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(LICENSE_API_TIMEOUT_MS),\n    });\n\n    const data = await response.json().catch(() => ({})) as { valid?: boolean; credits?: number };\n    const isValid = data.valid === true;\n    const credits = readFiniteCredits(data) ?? 0;\n\n    await cacheLicense(licenseKey, {\n      isValid,\n      credits,\n      cachedAt: new Date().toISOString(),\n    });\n\n    return { isValid, credits };\n  } catch {\n    return { isValid: false, credits: 0 };\n  }\n}\n\nasync function getCreditsBalance(licenseKey: string): Promise<{ credits: number; error?: string }> {\n  const apiBase = (process.env.NEXTJS_LICENSE_API_URL || DEFAULT_API_BASE_URL).replace(/\\/+$/, '');\n\n  try {\n    const response = await fetch(`${apiBase}/api/license/credits?license_key=${encodeURIComponent(licenseKey)}`, {\n      method: 'GET',\n      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(LICENSE_API_TIMEOUT_MS),\n    });\n\n    if (!response.ok) {\n      const errorData = await response.json().catch(() => ({})) as { error?: string };\n      return { credits: 0, error: errorData.error || `HTTP ${response.status}` };\n    }\n\n    const data = await response.json().catch(() => ({}));\n    const credits = readFiniteCredits(data);\n\n    if (credits === null) {\n      return { credits: 0, error: 'Invalid credits response' };\n    }\n\n    await cacheLicense(licenseKey, {\n      isValid: true,\n      credits,\n      cachedAt: new Date().toISOString(),\n    });\n\n    return { credits };\n  } catch (error) {\n    return { credits: 0, error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error) };\n  }\n}\n\nexport async function usageRoute(c: Context) {\n  const clientIP = getClientIP(c);\n\n  if (await isIPBlocked(clientIP)) {\n    return errorResponse(403, 'Access denied', 'Your IP has been temporarily blocked due to abuse');\n  }\n\n  // `account_key` is the canonical param; `license_key` is the legacy alias that\n  // installed native apps still send, so we accept either.\n  const licenseKey =\n    c.req.query('account_key') || c.req.query('license_key') || c.req.query('identifier')?.trim() || null;\n  const forceRefresh = c.req.query('force_refresh') === 'true';\n\n  if (licenseKey) {\n    let isValid = false;\n    let credits = 0;\n\n    if (forceRefresh) {\n      const cached = await getCachedLicense(licenseKey);\n      if (cached?.isValid) {\n        const balanceResult = await getCreditsBalance(licenseKey);\n        if (balanceResult.error) {\n          const validation = await validateLicenseAndGetCredits(licenseKey, true);\n          isValid = validation.isValid;\n          credits = validation.credits;\n        } else {\n          isValid = true;\n          credits = balanceResult.credits;\n        }\n      } else {\n        const validation = await validateLicenseAndGetCredits(licenseKey, true);\n        isValid = validation.isValid;\n        credits = validation.credits;\n      }\n    } else {\n      const validation = await validateLicenseAndGetCredits(licenseKey, false);\n      isValid = validation.isValid;\n      credits = validation.credits;\n    }\n\n    if (!isValid) {\n      return errorResponse(401, 'Invalid license key', 'The provided license key is invalid or expired');\n    }\n\n    const normalizedCredits = roundToTenth(credits);\n    const minutesRemaining = Math.floor(normalizedCredits / CREDITS_PER_MINUTE);\n\n    const response = {\n      credits_remaining: normalizedCredits,\n      minutes_remaining: minutesRemaining,\n      credits_per_minute: CREDITS_PER_MINUTE,\n      is_licensed: true,\n      is_trial: false,\n      is_anonymous: false,\n    };\n\n    return jsonResponse(response);\n  }\n\n  return errorResponse(401, 'License required', 'You must provide a valid license_key. HyperWhisper Cloud requires a license key.');\n}\n","numLines":154,"startLine":1,"totalLines":154}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"407ec843-e2dc-4eee-bf59-6b52866ae64d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{\n        isValid: true,\n        credits: data.credits_remaining,\n        cachedAt: new Date().toISOString(),\n      });\n    }\n  } catch (error) {\n    console.warn('POST /api/license/credits network error', {\n      error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),\n    });\n  }\n}\n\n// In-flight deduction tracking for graceful shutdown.\n// Call sites fire deductCredits() without awaiting (response latency), so a\n// Fly machine recycle (SIGTERM on deploy/scale-down) between the response\n// flush and the redis/license write would silently drop the charge. Every\n// deduction registers here so the SIGTERM handler can drain before exit.\nconst inFlightDeductions = new Set<Promise<number>>();\n\nexport async function drainPendingDeductions(timeoutMs: number): Promise<number> {\n  const pendingCount = inFlightDeductions.size;\n  if (pendingCount === 0) {\n    return 0;\n  }\n\n  const allSettled = Promise.allSettled([...inFlightDeductions]);\n  const timeout = new Promise<void>((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, timeoutMs));\n  await Promise.race([allSettled, timeout]);\n  return pendingCount;\n}\n\nexport function deductCredits(\n  auth: AuthContext,\n  costUsd: number,\n  metadata: Record<string, unknown>,\n  clientIP: string\n): Promise<number> {\n  const deduction = performDeduction(auth, costUsd, metadata, clientIP);\n  inFlightDeductions.add(deduction);\n  deduction\n    .catch(() => {}) // errors are logged inside performDeduction / by callers\n    .finally(() => inFlightDeductions.delete(deduction));\n  return deduction;\n}\n\nasync function performDeduction(\n  auth: AuthContext,\n  costUsd: number,\n  metadata: Record<string, unknown>,\n  _clientIP: string\n): Promise<number> {\n  const creditsUsed = creditsForCost(costUsd);\n\n  if (creditsUsed <= 0) {\n    return 0;\n  }\n\n  await recordLicenseUsage(auth.identifier, creditsUsed, metadata);\n  return creditsUsed;\n}\n","numLines":147,"startLine":1,"totalLines":147}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"13e3cfa4-be89-4e63-9a5d-5afde7b5b20d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{"parentUuid":"be8ad270-7061-4968-a410-9d192f558a57","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"77ec06fb-aafc-4d6b-b92c-3296ca279672","agentId":"a35f1a5a89ceb1af1","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01FDFYZ7b5qnTicfXbYyr4QJ","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport type { Context } from 'hono';\n2\timport { getConnInfo } from 'hono/bun';\n3\t\n4\texport function generateRequestId(): string {\n5\t  return crypto.randomUUID();\n6\t}\n7\t\n8\t// Fly.io stamps `Fly-Request-Id` on every request its edge proxy forwards, so\n9\t// it's a handy correlation id for logs. It is NOT a trust signal: any caller\n10\t// that reaches the Machine off-edge (6PN, private IPv6, colocated process)\n11\t// controls its own request headers and can forge this value, so it must never\n12\t// gate IP trust on its own.\n13\texport function getFlyRequestId(c: Context): string | undefined {\n14\t  return c.req.header('Fly-Request-Id')\n15\t    || c.req.header('Fly-Request-ID')\n16\t    || c.req.header('fly-request-id')\n17\t    || undefined;\n18\t}\n19\t\n20\t// True when the request reached this Machine directly over the private 6PN\n21\t// network (another org Machine, the Machine's own private IPv6, or a colocated\n22\t// process) instead of through Fly's public edge proxy. All Fly private IPv6\n23\t// addresses live under `fdaa::/16`, and 6PN connections bypass the proxy\n24\t// entirely — so a 6PN peer address is proof the request did NOT transit the\n25\t// edge. Unlike `Fly-Request-Id`/`Fly-Client-IP`, the TCP peer address is\n26\t// reported by the kernel and cannot be forged by the caller.\n27\tfunction isOffEdgePeer(c: Context): boolean {\n28\t  let peer: string | undefined;\n29\t  try {\n30\t    peer = getConnInfo(c).remote.address;\n31\t  } catch {\n32\t    // No connection info (e.g. unit tests via `app.request`, or non-Bun\n33\t    // runtimes). Fall back to header-based resolution rather than failing open\n34\t    // on a thrown error.\n35\t    return false;\n36\t  }\n37\t  if (!peer) {\n38\t    return false;\n39\t  }\n40\t  return peer.toLowerCase().startsWith('fdaa:');\n41\t}\n42\t\n43\t// Resolve the caller's IP for rate-limiting / IP-blocking.\n44\t//\n45\t// `Fly-Client-IP` and `X-Forwarded-For` are only trustworthy when the request\n46\t// came through Fly's public edge proxy, which strips any client-supplied\n47\t// `Fly-Client-IP` before forwarding. A caller that reaches the Machine off-edge\n48\t// (6PN, private IPv6, colocated process) controls every request header — it can\n49\t// forge both `Fly-Request-Id` and `Fly-Client-IP` — so header presence proves\n50\t// nothing. We instead key trust on the unforgeable TCP peer address: if the\n51\t// request arrived directly over 6PN we refuse to honor the headers and fall\n52\t// back to `'unknown'`, keeping per-IP daily quota and `ip_blocked` enforceable.\n53\texport function getClientIP(c: Context): string {\n54\t  if (isOffEdgePeer(c)) {\n55\t    return 'unknown';\n56\t  }\n57\t\n58\t  return c.req.header('Fly-Client-IP')\n59\t    || c.req.header('X-Forwarded-For')?.split(',')[0]?.trim()\n60\t    || 'unknown';\n61\t}\n62\t"}]},"uuid":"dd0b76d8-4b87-486b-8728-bb58ea2750e8","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:39:24.965Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/hyperwhisper-cloud/src/lib/request-id.ts","content":"import type { Context } from 'hono';\nimport { getConnInfo } from 'hono/bun';\n\nexport function generateRequestId(): string {\n  return crypto.randomUUID();\n}\n\n// Fly.io stamps `Fly-Request-Id` on every request its edge proxy forwards, so\n// it's a handy correlation id for logs. It is NOT a trust signal: any caller\n// that reaches the Machine off-edge (6PN, private IPv6, colocated process)\n// controls its own request headers and can forge this value, so it must never\n// gate IP trust on its own.\nexport function getFlyRequestId(c: Context): string | undefined {\n  return c.req.header('Fly-Request-Id')\n    || c.req.header('Fly-Request-ID')\n    || c.req.header('fly-request-id')\n    || undefined;\n}\n\n// True when the request reached this Machine directly over the private 6PN\n// network (another org Machine, the Machine's own private IPv6, or a colocated\n// process) instead of through Fly's public edge proxy. All Fly private IPv6\n// addresses live under `fdaa::/16`, and 6PN connections bypass the proxy\n// entirely — so a 6PN peer address is proof the request did NOT transit the\n// edge. Unlike `Fly-Request-Id`/`Fly-Client-IP`, the TCP peer address is\n// reported by the kernel and cannot be forged by the caller.\nfunction isOffEdgePeer(c: Context): boolean {\n  let peer: string | undefined;\n  try {\n    peer = getConnInfo(c).remote.address;\n  } catch {\n    // No connection info (e.g. unit tests via `app.request`, or non-Bun\n    // runtimes). Fall back to header-based resolution rather than failing open\n    // on a thrown error.\n    return false;\n  }\n  if (!peer) {\n    return false;\n  }\n  return peer.toLowerCase().startsWith('fdaa:');\n}\n\n// Resolve the caller's IP for rate-limiting / IP-blocking.\n//\n// `Fly-Client-IP` and `X-Forwarded-For` are only trustworthy when the request\n// came through Fly's public edge proxy, which strips any client-supplied\n// `Fly-Client-IP` before forwarding. A caller that reaches the Machine off-edge\n// (6PN, private IPv6, colocated process) controls every request header — it can\n// forge both `Fly-Request-Id` and `Fly-Client-IP` — so header presence proves\n// nothing. We instead key trust on the unforgeable TCP peer address: if the\n// request arrived directly over 6PN we refuse to honor the headers and fall\n// back to `'unknown'`, keeping per-IP daily quota and `ip_blocked` enforceable.\nexport function getClientIP(c: Context): string {\n  if (isOffEdgePeer(c)) {\n    return 'unknown';\n  }\n\n  return c.req.header('Fly-Client-IP')\n    || c.req.header('X-Forwarded-For')?.split(',')[0]?.trim()\n    || 'unknown';\n}\n","numLines":62,"startLine":1,"totalLines":62}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"be8ad270-7061-4968-a410-9d192f558a57","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{"parentUuid":"84aeba30-c263-41f0-b1b3-59ce5dc92b03","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"77ec06fb-aafc-4d6b-b92c-3296ca279672","agentId":"a35f1a5a89ceb1af1","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01PjyayE8B8yyvUajr3RhKfc","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t// RESPONSE HELPERS\n2\t// Standardized JSON responses with CORS headers\n3\t\n4\timport { CREDITS_PER_MINUTE } from './constants';\n5\t\n6\texport const CORS_HEADERS: Record<string, string> = {\n7\t  'Access-Control-Allow-Origin': '*',\n8\t  'Access-Control-Allow-Methods': 'GET, POST, OPTIONS',\n9\t  'Access-Control-Allow-Headers': 'Content-Type',\n10\t  'Access-Control-Max-Age': '86400',\n11\t};\n12\t\n13\texport function errorResponse(\n14\t  status: number,\n15\t  error: string,\n16\t  message: string,\n17\t  extra?: Record<string, unknown>\n18\t): Response {\n19\t  return new Response(\n20\t    JSON.stringify({ error, message, ...extra }),\n21\t    {\n22\t      status,\n23\t      headers: { ...CORS_HEADERS, 'content-type': 'application/json' },\n24\t    }\n25\t  );\n26\t}\n27\t\n28\texport function jsonResponse<T>(data: T, headers?: Record<string, string>): Response {\n29\t  return new Response(JSON.stringify(data), {\n30\t    status: 200,\n31\t    headers: { ...CORS_HEADERS, 'content-type': 'application/json', ...headers },\n32\t  });\n33\t}\n34\t\n35\t// Common error responses\n36\texport function licenseRequiredResponse(): Response {\n37\t  return errorResponse(401, 'License required', 'HyperWhisper Cloud requires a license key. This transcription requires approximately ${estimated.toFixed(1)} credits.`,\n50\t    {\n51\t      credits_remaining: balance,\n52\t      minutes_remaining: minutesRemaining,\n53\t      minutes_required: minutesRequired,\n54\t      credits_per_minute: CREDITS_PER_MINUTE,\n55\t    }\n56\t  );\n57\t}\n58\t\n59\texport function invalidContentTypeResponse(expected: string, received: string): Response {\n60\t  return errorResponse(400, 'Invalid Content-Type', `Content-Type must be ${expected}`, { received });\n61\t}\n62\t\n63\texport function missingContentLengthResponse(): Response {\n64\t  return errorResponse(400, 'Missing Content-Length', 'Content-Length header is required for streaming transcription');\n65\t}\n66\t\n67\texport function fileTooLargeResponse(actualBytes: number, maxBytes: number): Response {\n68\t  const actualMB = actualBytes / (1024 * 1024);\n69\t  const maxMB = maxBytes / (1024 * 1024);\n70\t\n71\t  return errorResponse(413, 'File too large',\n72\t    `Audio file must be ${maxMB.toFixed(0)} MB or smaller. Your image is ${actualMB.toFixed(2)} MB.`,\n86\t    {\n87\t      max_size_mb: Math.round(maxMB),\n88\t      actual_size_mb: parseFloat(actualMB.toFixed(2)),\n89\t    }\n90\t  );\n91\t}\n92\t"}]},"uuid":"00115287-f2bd-44ea-af6d-871aa92d0e20","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:39:25.435Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/hyperwhisper-cloud/src/lib/responses.ts","content":"// RESPONSE HELPERS\n// Standardized JSON responses with CORS headers\n\nimport { CREDITS_PER_MINUTE } from './constants';\n\nexport const CORS_HEADERS: Record<string, string> = {\n  'Access-Control-Allow-Origin': '*',\n  'Access-Control-Allow-Methods': 'GET, POST, OPTIONS',\n  'Access-Control-Allow-Headers': 'Content-Type',\n  'Access-Control-Max-Age': '86400',\n};\n\nexport function errorResponse(\n  status: number,\n  error: string,\n  message: string,\n  extra?: Record<string, unknown>\n): Response {\n  return new Response(\n    JSON.stringify({ error, message, ...extra }),\n    {\n      status,\n      headers: { ...CORS_HEADERS, 'content-type': 'application/json' },\n    }\n  );\n}\n\nexport function jsonResponse<T>(data: T, headers?: Record<string, string>): Response {\n  return new Response(JSON.stringify(data), {\n    status: 200,\n    headers: { ...CORS_HEADERS, 'content-type': 'application/json', ...headers },\n  });\n}\n\n// Common error responses\nexport function licenseRequiredResponse(): Response {\n  return errorResponse(401, 'License required', 'HyperWhisper Cloud requires a license key. Add credits to get a key, or use your own API key for free.');\n}\n\nexport function invalidLicenseResponse(): Response {\n  return errorResponse(401, 'Invalid license', 'The provided license key is invalid or expired');\n}\n\nexport function insufficientCreditsResponse(balance: number, estimated: number): Response {\n  const minutesRemaining = Math.floor(balance / CREDITS_PER_MINUTE);\n  const minutesRequired = Math.ceil(estimated / CREDITS_PER_MINUTE);\n\n  return errorResponse(402, 'Insufficient credits',\n    `You have ${balance.toFixed(1)} credits remaining. This transcription requires approximately ${estimated.toFixed(1)} credits.`,\n    {\n      credits_remaining: balance,\n      minutes_remaining: minutesRemaining,\n      minutes_required: minutesRequired,\n      credits_per_minute: CREDITS_PER_MINUTE,\n    }\n  );\n}\n\nexport function invalidContentTypeResponse(expected: string, received: string): Response {\n  return errorResponse(400, 'Invalid Content-Type', `Content-Type must be ${expected}`, { received });\n}\n\nexport function missingContentLengthResponse(): Response {\n  return errorResponse(400, 'Missing Content-Length', 'Content-Length header is required for streaming transcription');\n}\n\nexport function fileTooLargeResponse(actualBytes: number, maxBytes: number): Response {\n  const actualMB = actualBytes / (1024 * 1024);\n  const maxMB = maxBytes / (1024 * 1024);\n\n  return errorResponse(413, 'File too large',\n    `Audio file must be ${maxMB.toFixed(0)} MB or smaller. Your file is ${actualMB.toFixed(2)} MB.`,\n    {\n      max_size_mb: Math.round(maxMB),\n      actual_size_mb: parseFloat(actualMB.toFixed(2)),\n    }\n  );\n}\n\nexport function imageTooLargeResponse(actualBytes: number, maxBytes: number): Response {\n  const actualMB = actualBytes / (1024 * 1024);\n  const maxMB = maxBytes / (1024 * 1024);\n\n  return errorResponse(413, 'Image too large',\n    `Image must be ${maxMB.toFixed(0)} MB or smaller. Your image is ${actualMB.toFixed(2)} MB.`,\n    {\n      max_size_mb: Math.round(maxMB),\n      actual_size_mb: parseFloat(actualMB.toFixed(2)),\n    }\n  );\n}\n","numLines":92,"startLine":1,"totalLines":92}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"84aeba30-c263-41f0-b1b3-59ce5dc92b03","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

Checked against\n19\t// Content-Length BEFORE buffering so an oversized upload is rejected early\n20\t// rather than after buffering up to MAX_AUDIO_SIZE_BYTES on the Fly machine.\n21\texport const GEMINI_INLINE_MAX_BYTES = 14 * 1024 * 1024;\n22\t\n23\t// OpenAI hard-rejects audio over 25 MB with a 400. Gate on Content-Length\n24\t// BEFORE buffering so an oversized upload is rejected early as a 413 rather\n25\t// than after allocating the buffer. Shared with the adapter (defense-in-depth).\n26\texport const OPENAI_INLINE_MAX_BYTES = 25 * 1024 * 1024;\n27\t\n28\t// Azure MAI-Transcribe accepts payloads up to ~300 MB across its multipart\n29\t// upload — beyond that the gateway 413s with a generic \"Request entity too\n30\t// large\" before any model code runs.\n31\texport const AZURE_MAI_MAX_BYTES = 300 * 1024 * 1024;\n32\t\n33\t// `/assistant` coarse total-body guard, checked against Content-Length BEFORE\n34\t// buffering so a multi-hundred-MB upload is rejected early (the body is fully\n35\t// buffered by formData() on a 1 GB Fly machine). Finer per-image / per-messages\n36\t// caps below bound Anthropic vision spend after buffering.\n37\texport const MAX_ASSISTANT_BODY_BYTES = 25 * 1024 * 1024; // 25 MB\n38\t// Multipart `image` file cap.\n39\texport const MAX_ASSISTANT_IMAGE_BYTES = 10 * 1024 * 1024; // 10 MB\n40\t\n41\t// Fly.io's dynamic request routing (`fly-replay`) only honours the replay\n42\t// header for requests with bodies ≤ 1 MB. Larger requests are silently\n43\t// executed in the original region instead of being replayed. Use 900 KB as\n44\t// the gate to leave headroom for any Fly-side accounting.\n45\t// Ref: https://fly.io/docs/networking/dynamic-request-routing/\n46\texport const FLY_REPLAY_MAX_BODY_BYTES = 900_000;\n47\t\n48\t// Assistant (vision LLM) limits — guard against unbounded Anthropic spend from\n49\t// oversized or numerous client-supplied images. Clients embed images either as\n50\t// a multipart `image` file or inline as data:image/...;base64,... blocks inside\n51\t// the `messages` JSON; both paths must be capped.\n52\t//\n53\t// Total serialized `messages` payload. Sized to admit ASSISTANT_MAX_IMAGES inline\n54\t// images at ASSISTANT_MAX_INLINE_IMAGE_BYTES decoded (base64 ~4/3 expansion ≈\n55\t// 5.3 MB) plus text headroom, while still bounding inline-base64 abuse.\n56\texport const ASSISTANT_MAX_MESSAGES_BYTES = 6 * 1024 * 1024;\n57\t// Decoded size of any single inline base64 image forwarded to Anthropic.\n58\texport const ASSISTANT_MAX_INLINE_IMAGE_BYTES = 2 * 1024 * 1024;\n59\t// Maximum number of images forwarded to Anthropic per request (multipart +\n60\t// inline combined). Screen-aware assistant requests use one screenshot.\n61\texport const ASSISTANT_MAX_IMAGES = 2;\n62\t\n63\t// API base\n64\texport const DEFAULT_API_BASE_URL = 'https://www.hyperwhisper.com';\n65\t\n66\t// Hard timeout for every call to the Next.js license API (validate / credits).\n67\t// Without it a hung upstream (Vercel cold start, downstream DB stall, edge\n68\t// retry storm) leaves an unresolved `fetch` Promise pinning the request scope,\n69\t// which bloats the Bun worker until Fly OOM-kills it. 10s tolerates a cold\n70\t// start while still bounding the hang well under client/edge timeouts.\n71\texport const LICENSE_API_TIMEOUT_MS = 10_000;\n72\t"}]},"uuid":"c69948b5-36a6-49ea-a8fe-b38c82a1e181","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:39:25.919Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/hyperwhisper-cloud/src/lib/constants.ts","content":"// SHARED CONSTANTS\n\n// Credit system\nexport const CREDITS_PER_MINUTE = 6.3; // Derived from production usage logs\n\n// License cache TTL (seconds)\nexport const LICENSE_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS = 60 * 60; // 1 hour for valid and invalid keys\n\n// Audio limits\nexport const MAX_AUDIO_SIZE_BYTES = 2 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024; // 2GB\nexport const BYTES_PER_MINUTE_ESTIMATE = 480_000; // 64kbps encoded audio\n\n// Google Speech V2 sync `recognize` inline cap (also re-exported from\n// `providers/google-chirp.ts` for back-compat with existing call sites).\nexport const GOOGLE_CHIRP_INLINE_MAX_BYTES = 9_500_000;\n\n// Gemini inline-audio cap: total request (incl. base64) must stay under 20 MB\n// and base64 inflates ~33%, so raw audio is capped at ~14 MB. Checked against\n// Content-Length BEFORE buffering so an oversized upload is rejected early\n// rather than after buffering up to MAX_AUDIO_SIZE_BYTES on the Fly machine.\nexport const GEMINI_INLINE_MAX_BYTES = 14 * 1024 * 1024;\n\n// OpenAI hard-rejects audio over 25 MB with a 400. Shared with the adapter (defense-in-depth).\nexport const OPENAI_INLINE_MAX_BYTES = 25 * 1024 * 1024;\n\n// Azure MAI-Transcribe accepts payloads up to ~300 MB across its multipart\n// upload — beyond that the gateway 413s with a generic \"Request entity too\n// large\" before any model code runs.\nexport const AZURE_MAI_MAX_BYTES = 300 * 1024 * 1024;\n\n// `/assistant` coarse total-body guard, checked against Content-Length BEFORE\n// buffering so a multi-hundred-MB upload is rejected early (the body is fully\n// buffered by formData() on a 1 GB Fly machine). Finer per-image / per-messages\n// caps below bound Anthropic vision spend after buffering.\nexport const MAX_ASSISTANT_BODY_BYTES = 25 * 1024 * 1024; // 25 MB\n// Multipart `image` file cap.\nexport const MAX_ASSISTANT_IMAGE_BYTES = 10 * 1024 * 1024; // 10 MB\n\n// Fly.io's dynamic request routing (`fly-replay`) only honours the replay\n// header for requests with bodies ≤ 1 MB. Clients embed images either as\n// a multipart `image` file or inline as data:image/...;base64,... blocks inside\n// the `messages` JSON; both paths must be capped.\n//\n// Total serialized `messages` payload. Sized to admit ASSISTANT_MAX_IMAGES inline\n// images at ASSISTANT_MAX_INLINE_IMAGE_BYTES decoded (base64 ~4/3 expansion ≈\n// 5.3 MB) plus text headroom, while still bounding inline-base64 abuse.\nexport const ASSISTANT_MAX_MESSAGES_BYTES = 6 * 1024 * 1024;\n// Decoded size of any single inline base64 image forwarded to Anthropic.\nexport const ASSISTANT_MAX_INLINE_IMAGE_BYTES = 2 * 1024 * 1024;\n// Maximum number of images forwarded to Anthropic per request (multipart +\n// inline combined). Screen-aware assistant requests use one screenshot.\nexport const ASSISTANT_MAX_IMAGES = 2;\n\n// API base\nexport const DEFAULT_API_BASE_URL = 'https://www.hyperwhisper.com';\n\n// Hard timeout for every call to the Next.js license API (validate / credits).\n// Without it a hung upstream (Vercel cold start, downstream DB stall, edge\n// retry storm) leaves an unresolved `fetch` Promise pinning the request scope,\n// which bloats the Bun worker until Fly OOM-kills it. 10s tolerates a cold\n// start while still bounding the hang well under client/edge timeouts.\nexport const LICENSE_API_TIMEOUT_MS = 10_000;\n","numLines":72,"startLine":1,"totalLines":72}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"56ce1eaf-a75c-412f-9aa7-f814263e8697","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{"parentUuid":"22497377-c5a0-44ff-a8ed-d0ca96abff9d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"77ec06fb-aafc-4d6b-b92c-3296ca279672","agentId":"a35f1a5a89ceb1af1","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01E8eneUJyv9N8j8va769nnF","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t// GENERAL UTILITY HELPERS\n2\t// Shared helpers for type guards, rounding, and retry operations\n3\t\n4\t/**\n5\t * Type guard for plain object values\n6\t */\n7\texport function isRecord(value: unknown): value is Record<string, unknown> {\n8\t  return typeof value === 'object' && value !== null;\n9\t}\n10\t\n11\t/**\n12\t * Safely read the body text from a Response without throwing\n13\t */\n14\texport async function safeReadText(response: Response): Promise<string | undefined> {\n15\t  try {\n16\t    return await response.text();\n17\t  } catch {\n18\t    return undefined;\n19\t  }\n20\t}\n21\t\n22\t/**\n23\t * Round a numeric value to the nearest tenth, guarding against NaN/Infinity.\n24\t */\n25\texport function roundToTenth(value: number): number {\n26\t  if (!Number.isFinite(value)) {\n27\t    return 0;\n28\t  }\n29\t\n30\t  const rounded = Math.round((value + Number.EPSILON) * 10) / 10;\n31\t  return Math.abs(rounded) < Number.EPSILON ? 0 : rounded;\n32\t}\n33\t\n34\t/**\n35\t * Round a numeric value up to the next tenth, ensuring non-negative output.\n36\t */\n37\texport function roundUpToTenth(value: number): number {\n38\t  if (!Number.isFinite(value)) {\n39\t    return 0;\n40\t  }\n41\t\n42\t  const rounded = Math.ceil((value - Number.EPSILON) * 10) / 10;\n43\t  return rounded <= 0 ? 0 : rounded;\n44\t}\n45\t\n46\t/**\n47\t * Retry configuration options\n48\t */\n49\texport interface RetryOptions {\n50\t  /** Maximum number of retry attempts (default: 3) */\n51\t  maxRetries?: number;\n52\t  /** Initial delay in milliseconds (default: 1000ms) */\n53\t  initialDelayMs?: number;\n54\t  /** Backoff multiplier for exponential delay (default: 2) */\n55\t  backoffMultiplier?: number;\n56\t  /** Optional callback invoked before each retry attempt */\n57\t  onRetry?: (attempt: number, error: Error, delayMs: number) => void;\n58\t}\n59\t\n60\t/**\n61\t * Retry an async operation with exponential backoff\n62\t */\n63\texport async function retryWithBackoff<T>(\n64\t  fn: () => Promise<T>,\n65\t  options: RetryOptions = {}\n66\t): Promise<T> {\n67\t  const {\n68\t    maxRetries = 3,\n69\t    initialDelayMs = 1000,\n70\t    backoffMultiplier = 2,\n71\t    onRetry,\n72\t  } = options;\n73\t\n74\t  let lastError: Error;\n75\t\n76\t  for (let attempt = 0; attempt <= maxRetries; attempt++) {\n77\t    try {\n78\t      return await fn();\n79\t    } catch (error) {\n80\t      lastError = error instanceof Error ? 0 : rounded;\n}\n\n/**\n * Round a numeric value up to the next tenth, ensuring non-negative output.\n */\nexport function roundUpToTenth(value: number): number {\n  if (!Number.isFinite(value)) {\n    return 0;\n  }\n\n  const rounded = Math.ceil((value - Number.EPSILON) * 10) / 10;\n  return rounded <= 0 ? 0 : rounded;\n}\n\n/**\n * Retry configuration options\n */\nexport interface RetryOptions {\n  /** Maximum number of retry attempts (default: 3) */\n  maxRetries?: number;\n  /** Initial delay in milliseconds (default: 1000ms) */\n  initialDelayMs?: number;\n  /** Backoff multiplier for exponential delay (default: 2) */\n  backoffMultiplier?: number;\n  /** Optional callback invoked before each retry attempt */\n  onRetry?: (attempt: number, error: Error, delayMs: number) => void;\n}\n\n/**\n * Retry an async operation with exponential backoff\n */\nexport async function retryWithBackoff<T>(\n  fn: () => Promise<T>,\n  options: RetryOptions = {}\n): Promise<T> {\n  const {\n    maxRetries = 3,\n    initialDelayMs = 1000,\n    backoffMultiplier = 2,\n    onRetry,\n  } = options;\n\n  let lastError: Error;\n\n  for (let attempt = 0; attempt <= maxRetries; attempt++) {\n    try {\n      return await fn();\n    } catch (error) {\n      lastError = error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error));\n\n      if (attempt === maxRetries) {\n        throw lastError;\n      }\n\n      const delayMs = initialDelayMs * Math.pow(backoffMultiplier, attempt);\n\n      if (onRetry) {\n        onRetry(attempt + 1, lastError, delayMs);\n      }\n\n      await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, delayMs));\n    }\n  }\n\n  throw lastError!;\n}\n","numLines":98,"startLine":1,"totalLines":98}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"22497377-c5a0-44ff-a8ed-d0ca96abff9d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

RFC 3986 query strings have no such rule, and neither\n5\t// platform client form-encodes: a vocabulary term like `C++` sent as\n6\t// `initial_prompt=C%2B%2B` (or even a raw `C++`) must arrive as `C++`, not\n7\t// `C  `. This helper re-parses the query string manually and decodes with\n8\t// `decodeURIComponent`, which leaves `+` alone.\n9\t//\n10\t// Scope: used by the /transcribe route only (see routes/transcribe.ts). Other\n11\t// routes keep `c.req.query()` — their params (keys, ids) never carry `+`.\n12\t\n13\t/**\n14\t * Read a single query parameter from `url`, percent-decoding the value while\n15\t * preserving literal `+`. Returns the FIRST occurrence, `undefined` when the\n16\t * parameter is absent or its value is empty (preserving the callers'\n17\t * `|| undefined` semantics), and the raw (undecoded) value when the value\n18\t * contains a malformed percent-escape.\n19\t */\n20\texport function rawQuery(url: string, name: string): string | undefined {\n21\t  const queryStart = url.indexOf('?');\n22\t  if (queryStart === -1) return undefined;\n23\t\n24\t  // Drop a fragment if present (defensive; clients don't send one).\n25\t  const hashStart = url.indexOf('#', queryStart);\n26\t  const query = url.slice(queryStart + 1, hashStart === -1 ? undefined : hashStart);\n27\t\n28\t  for (const pair of query.split('&')) {\n29\t    const eq = pair.indexOf('=');\n30\t    const rawKey = eq === -1 ? pair : pair.slice(0, eq);\n31\t\n32\t    let key: string;\n33\t    try {\n34\t      key = decodeURIComponent(rawKey);\n35\t    } catch {\n36\t      key = rawKey;\n37\t    }\n38\t    if (key !== name) continue;\n39\t\n40\t    const rawValue = eq === -1 ? '' : pair.slice(eq + 1);\n41\t    let value: string;\n42\t    try {\n43\t      value = decodeURIComponent(rawValue);\n44\t    } catch {\n45\t      // Malformed escape (e.g. a stray `%`): fall back to the raw value rather\n46\t      // than throwing a 500 for one bad byte.\n47\t      value = rawValue;\n48\t    }\n49\t    return value === '' ? undefined : value;\n50\t  }\n51\t\n52\t  return undefined;\n53\t}\n54\t"}]},"uuid":"653c9233-abb9-41f8-a650-f6b5f8050173","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:39:27.320Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/hyperwhisper-cloud/src/lib/query.ts","content":"// Raw query-string parsing that preserves literal `+`.\n//\n// Hono's `c.req.query()` decodes query values with an extra `+` → space step\n// (HTML-form semantics). RFC 3986 query strings have no such rule, and neither\n// platform client form-encodes: a vocabulary term like `C++` sent as\n// `initial_prompt=C%2B%2B` (or even a raw `C++`) must arrive as `C++`, not\n// `C  `. This helper re-parses the query string manually and decodes with\n// `decodeURIComponent`, which leaves `+` alone.\n//\n// Scope: used by the /transcribe route only (see routes/transcribe.ts). Other\n// routes keep `c.req.query()` — their params (keys, ids) never carry `+`.\n\n/**\n * Read a single query parameter from `url`, percent-decoding the value while\n * preserving literal `+`. Returns the FIRST occurrence, `undefined` when the\n * parameter is absent or its value is empty (preserving the callers'\n * `|| undefined` semantics), and the raw (undecoded) value when the value\n * contains a malformed percent-escape.\n */\nexport function rawQuery(url: string, name: string): string | undefined {\n  const queryStart = url.indexOf('?');\n  if (queryStart === -1) return undefined;\n\n  // Drop a fragment if present (defensive; clients don't send one).\n  const hashStart = url.indexOf('#', queryStart);\n  const query = url.slice(queryStart + 1, hashStart === -1 ? undefined : hashStart);\n\n  for (const pair of query.split('&')) {\n    const eq = pair.indexOf('=');\n    const rawKey = eq === -1 ? pair : pair.slice(0, eq);\n\n    let key: string;\n    try {\n      key = decodeURIComponent(rawKey);\n    } catch {\n      key = rawKey;\n    }\n    if (key !== name) continue;\n\n    const rawValue = eq === -1 ? '' : pair.slice(eq + 1);\n    let value: string;\n    try {\n      value = decodeURIComponent(rawValue);\n    } catch {\n      // Malformed escape (e.g. a stray `%`): fall back to the raw value rather\n      // than throwing a 500 for one bad byte.\n      value = rawValue;\n    }\n    return value === '' ? undefined : value;\n  }\n\n  return undefined;\n}\n","numLines":54,"startLine":1,"totalLines":54}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"890f7462-53ae-4fbb-ba1c-c905b75226c3","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}
